Table
2
gives the numerical results of different solvers. We make the following observations. (i) Similar to Table
1
for the (
BQP
) problem, IPMs can solve small and medium problems (
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28720
) to high accuracy, although the runtime grows significantly from
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720
(about
\mathchar 28721
second) to
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28720
(about
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720
seconds). (ii) Both
CDCS
and
SDPNAL+
are able to solve all SDPs to high accuracy and certify the tightness of the second-order relaxation (despite that
CDCS
only attained medium accuracy for
\#\mathchar 28722
at
\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28720
). However,
SDPNAL+
is significantly faster than
CDCS
, in most cases
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720
-
\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28720
times faster. Compared to Table
1
, these results suggest that the (
Q4S
) relaxation is easier to solve than the (
BQP
) relaxation, perhaps because (
Q4S
) only has a single unit-norm constraint. (iii)
SketchyCGAL
, however, failed to solve most of the SDPs to high accuracy, despite taking more time than
CDCS
and
SDPNAL+
. (iv) Our solver
STRIDE
achieved similar performance compared to
SDPNAL+
. Although
STRIDE
can be slightly slower than
SDPNAL+
, it generally attained higher accuracy than
SDPNAL+
.

5.3
Outlier-robust Wahba problem

Consider the problem of finding the best 3D rotation to align two sets of 3D points while explicitly tolerating
outliers

\displaystyle\min_{\mathchar 29041\mathchar 12850{\cal\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28723}}\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006}\min\left\{{{\left\delimiter 0\tilde{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041\mathchar 8718\tilde{\mathchar 29047}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8718\mathchar 29041^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\right\delimiter 0^{\mathchar 28722}\over\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 28722}}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\right\}

(51)

where
\mathchar 29041\mathchar 12850{\cal\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28723}
is the
unit quaternion
parametrization of a 3D rotation,
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 28723}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 28723}}\delimiter 84054785_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006}
are given
\mathchar 29006
pairs of 3D points (often normalized to have unit norm),
\tilde{\mathchar 29050}\triangleq\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29050^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720\delimiter 84267779^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 28724}}
denotes the zero-homogenization of a 3D vector
\mathchar 29050
,
\mathchar 29041^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\triangleq\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28724}\delimiter 84267779^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}
is the inverse quaternion, “
\mathchar 8718
” denotes the quaternion product defined as

\displaystyle\mathchar 29041\mathchar 8718\mathchar 29040\triangleq\left\delimiter 67482370\begin{array}[]{cccc}\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28724}&\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28723}&\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28722}&\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28721}\\
\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28723}&\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28724}&\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28721}&\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28722}\\
\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28722}&\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28721}&\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28724}&\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28723}\\
\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28721}&\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28722}&\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28723}&\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 28724}\end{array}\right\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 29040\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 568\mathchar 29041\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29040\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 28724}}\mathchar 24891

\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12606\mathchar 28720
is a given threshold that determines the maximum
inlier
residual, and
\min\left\{\mathchar 8705\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8705\right\}
realizes the so-called
truncated least squares
(TLS) cost function in robust estimation
Antonante20arxiv-outlier
. Intuitively, the term
\mathchar 29041\mathchar 8718\tilde{\mathchar 29047}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8718\mathchar 29041^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
is the rotated copy of
\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}
, and the
\mathchar 352_{\mathchar 28722}
norm in (
51
) measures the Euclidean distance between
\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}
and
\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}
after rotation (a metric for the goodness of fit). Problem (
51
) therefore seeks to find the best 3D rotation that minimizes the sum of (normalized) squared Euclidean distances between
\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}
and
\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}
while preventing outliers from damaging the estimation via the usage of the TLS cost function, which assigns a constant value to those pairs of points that cannot be aligned well (
i.e.,
outliers). A pictorial description of the outlier-robust Wahba problem is presented in Fig.
2
. Problem (
51
) is nonsmooth, but can be equivalently reformulated as

\min_{\begin{\mathchar29043\mathchar29045\mathchar29026\mathchar29025\mathchar29042\mathchar29042\mathchar29025\mathchar29049}{\mathchar29027}\mathchar 29041\mathchar 12850{\cal\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28723}\mathchar 24891\\
\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12850\{\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006\end{\mathchar29043\mathchar29045\mathchar29026\mathchar29025\mathchar29042\mathchar29042\mathchar29025\mathchar29049}}\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006}{{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29033}\over\mathchar 28722}}{{\left\delimiter 0\tilde{\mathchar 29050}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 29041\mathchar 8718\tilde{\mathchar 29047}_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8718\mathchar 29041^{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\right\delimiter 0^{\mathchar 28722}\over\mathchar 28940_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 28722}}}\mathchar 8235{{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29033}\over\mathchar 28722}}

(Wahba)

by introducing
\mathchar 29006
binary variables
\{\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29033}\}
that expose the combinatorial nature. Each
\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29033}
acts as the selection variable for determining whether the
\mathchar 29033
-th pair of 3D points
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785
is an inlier or an outlier. Problem (
Wahba
) is a fundamental problem in aerospace, robotics and computer vision, and is the rotation subproblem in point cloud registration
Yang20TRO-teaser
;
Yang19rss-teaser
.

Figure 2:
An example of the outlier-robust (
Wahba
) problem. (a) Four pairs of 3D points
\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785
lying around a unit sphere, with one of the pairs being an outlier that cannot be aligned well by a 3D rotation. (b) A truncated least squares (TLS) cost function compared with a least squares cost function. The TLS cost function prevents the outlier from contaminating the estimation problem by assigning a constant cost to the outlier. Adapted from
Yang19ICCV-wahba
.

To solve (
Wahba
) to global optimality, Yang and Carlone
Yang19ICCV-wahba
proposed the following semidefinite relaxation that was empirically shown to be always tight. Let
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29041^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29006}\delimiter 84267779^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29006
, be the variable of the nonlinear programming problem (
Wahba
), construct

\displaystyle\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29041^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29041^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29006}\mathchar 29041^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\delimiter 84267779^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29006\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28724

(57)

as the sparse set of monomials in
\mathchar 29048
of degree up to 2 (a technique that was dubbed
binary cloning
), and then build
\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}
as the
sparse
moment matrix. Because of the binary constraint
\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721
, it can be easily seen that: (i) the diagonal
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 8706\mathchar 28724
blocks of
\mathchar 29016
are all identical (
\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 29041\mathchar 29041^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29041\mathchar 29041^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}
), and (ii) the off-diagonal
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 8706\mathchar 28724
blocks are symmetric (
\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 29041\mathchar 29041^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 12850\mathbb{\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28724}
). Because of the unit quaternion constraint,
\mathchar 29016
satisfies
\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29042}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29006\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721
. Therefore, this leads to a semidefinite relaxation of size

\displaystyle\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\mathchar 29006\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28724\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 29006\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28723\mathchar 29006\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29006\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

(58)

Compared to the dense second-order moment relaxation in Section
5.1
and
5.2
, this sparse second-order relaxation is much more manageable, and the largest
\mathchar 29006
whose relaxation was successfully solved by interior point method was
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720

Yang19ICCV-wahba
. This sparse second-order relaxation scheme has been shown as a general framework for certifiable outlier-robust machine perception
Yang20neurips-onering
.

Here we show the scalability of our solver by solving instances of (
Wahba
) up to
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720
and obtaining the globally optimal solution. At each
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720
,
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720
, we generate three random instances of the (
Wahba
) problem as follows. (i) We draw a random 3D rotation
\mathchar 29010\mathchar 12850\mathrm{\mathchar 29011\mathchar 29007}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28723\delimiter 84054785
(a rotation matrix can be converted from and to a unit quaternion easily); (ii) we simulate
\mathchar 29006
3D unit vectors
\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006
uniformly on the unit sphere; (iii) we generate

\displaystyle\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29010\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28943_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 28943_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12824{\cal\mathchar 29006}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28720\mathchar 314\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28721^{\mathchar 28722}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006

(59)

by rotating
\mathchar 29047_{\mathchar 29033}
and adding Gaussian noise; (iv) we replace
\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28720\%
of the
\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}
’s by random unit vectors on the sphere so that they do not follow the generative model (
59
) and are considered as outliers. We then use
SDPT3
,
MOSEK
,
CDCS
,
SketchyCGAL
,
SDPNAL+
, and
STRIDE
to solve the SDP relaxations. For
STRIDE
, we use
Manopt
with a trust region solver as the
nlp
method for solving the nonlinear programming (
Wahba
). Specifically,
\mathchar 29041\mathchar 12850{\cal\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 28723}
is modeled as a sphere manifold, and
\mathchar 28946\mathchar 12850\{\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\}^{\mathchar 29006}
is modeled as an oblique manifold of size
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29006
(an oblique manifold of size
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29037
is the set of matrices of size
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29037
with unit-norm columns), and the problem is treated as an unconstrained problem on the product of two manifolds. To round hypotheses from a moment matrix
\mathchar 29016
, we follow

\displaystyle\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349{{\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29041\delimiter 84267779\over\left\delimiter 0\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29041\delimiter 84267779\right\delimiter 0}}\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29034}^{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathrm{\mathchar 29043\mathchar 29031\mathchar 29038}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 29041\delimiter 84267779\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 24891\;\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006\mathchar 24891

(60)

where we first perform spectral decomposition of
\mathchar 29016
with eigenvalues in nonincreasing order, then round
\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 29033}
by normalizing the corresponding entries of
\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}
to have unit norm, and finally generate
\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29034}^{\mathchar 29033}
by taking the sign of the dot product between the rounded
\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 29033}
and the entries of
\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}
corresponding to each
\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 29041
block (the rationale for using this
rounding
method is easily seen from (
57
) where

\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29034}^{\mathchar 29033}
is identified with
\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28946_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785
for the rounded
\mathchar 29041_{\mathchar 29033}
with unit norm). We generate
\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28725
hypotheses by rounding
\mathchar 28725
eigenvectors from the moment matrix. We set
\mathchar 29005_{\mathchar 29026}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29006\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\mathchar 12349\mathrm{\mathchar 29044\mathchar 29042}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29016\right\delimiter 84054785
to compute
\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 29043}
as in (
46
).

Table
3
gives the numerical results for different solvers. Notice that at
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720
, we increased the maximum runtime of
SketchyCGAL
and
SDPNAL+
to be
\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720
seconds for a fair comparison with
STRIDE
. We make the following observations. (i) IPMs can solve small and medium problems (
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720
) to high accuracy and certify global optimality of the POP solutions. However, their runtime grows quickly and they cannot scale to problems with
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720
. (ii)
CDCS
,
SketchyCGAL
and
SDPNAL+
perform poorly on this problem. Notably,
CDCS
and
SketchyCGAL
failed on all instances and they cannot certify global optimality and tightness of the relaxation.
SDPNAL+
succeeded on problems with
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28720
but failed to attain high accuracy for all other problems. Comparing Table
3
with Tables
1
-
2
, the degraded performance of
CDCS
and
SDPNAL+
seems to suggest that sparse relaxations are more challenging to solve than dense relaxations. (iii)
STRIDE
was able to solve all SDP instances to high accuracy. Particularly, for
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720
,
STRIDE
achieved similar accuracy compared to
MOSEK
, while being
\mathchar 28723
times faster at
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28720
times faster at
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720
. For
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12821\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720
,
STRIDE
is the only solver than can attain high accuracy and certify global optimality and tightness (despite taking much less time than the other solvers).

STRIDE
with Domain-Specific Primal Initialization
. In Section
3.1
, we mentioned that
STRIDE
can benefit from domain-specific primal initialization. We now use the (
Wahba
) problem to support our claim. Although the (
Wahba
) problem is a combinatorial problem with binary variables, Yang et al.
Yang20ral-GNC
have designed a heuristic method called
graduated non-convexity
(
GNC
) that can solve (
Wahba
) to global optimality with high probability of success (note that
GNC
only outputs a solution without optimality certificate). Therefore, we first use
GNC
to solve the combinatorial (
Wahba
) problem and then use its solution as a primal initialization for
STRIDE
. Particularly, let
\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29041}\mathchar 24891\hat{\mathchar 28946}\delimiter 84054785
be the output of
GNC
, we input a rank-one point
\mathchar 29016^{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67482370\hat{\mathchar 29048}\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 67482370\hat{\mathchar 29048}\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}
to
STRIDE
, where
\delimiter 67482370\hat{\mathchar 29048}\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}
is computed from (
57
) with
\hat{\mathchar 29048}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 29041}\mathchar 24891\hat{\mathchar 28946}\delimiter 84054785
. The last column of Table
3
shows the numerical results for
STRIDE
with primal initialization supplied by
GNC
. We can see that the
GNC
primal initialization gives
STRIDE
an additional
\mathchar 28722
-
\mathchar 28723
times speedup.

Outlier-Robust Wahba Problem on Real Data
:
To show the practical usefulness of
STRIDE
, we test it on two applications of the Wahba problem on real data. The first application is image stitching on PASSTA
Meneghetti15SCIA-stitching
shown in Fig.
3
(a). Given two images taken by the same camera with an unknown relative rotation, we first use SURF
Bay06eccv-surf
to establish
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28727\mathchar 28720
putative keypoint matches, and then use
STRIDE
to solve the SDP relaxation of the Wahba problem (
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28728\mathchar 28724\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28725\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28726\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28721
) to estimate the relative rotation and stitch the two images.
STRIDE
obtains the globally optimal solution (
\max\{\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 29031}\}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28726\mathchar 314\mathchar 28722\mathrm{\mathchar 29029}{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28723}
,
\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28727\mathchar 314\mathchar 28728\mathrm{\mathchar 29029}{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28724}
) in
\mathchar 28727\mathchar 28729
seconds. The second application is point cloud registration on 3DMatch
Zeng17cvpr-3dmatch
shown in Fig.
3
(b). Given two point clouds with an unknown relative rotation, we first use FPFH
Rusu09icra-fast3Dkeypoints
and ROBIN
Shi21icra-robin
to establish
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28728
keypoint matches, and then use
STRIDE
to solve the SDP relaxation (
\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28726\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28726\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28729\mathchar 28727
) to estimate the rotation and register the point clouds.
STRIDE
obtains the globally optimal solution (
\max\{\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 29040}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 29028}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 29031}\}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 314\mathchar 28726\mathrm{\mathchar 29029}{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720}
,
\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 314\mathchar 28728\mathrm{\mathchar 29029}{\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28723}
) in
\mathchar 28725\mathchar 28723
seconds.

(a) Image stitching on PASSTA
Meneghetti15SCIA-stitching
.

(b) Point cloud registration on 3DMatch
Zeng17cvpr-3dmatch
.

Figure 3:
STRIDE
solves outlier-robust Wahba problems on real datasets to global optimality.

5.4
Nearest structured rank deficient matrices

Let
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}
be positive integers with
\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}
, and let
{\cal\mathchar 29004}\mathchar 12346{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 29006}}\mathchar 12833{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}}
be an affine map. Consider finding the nearest structured rank deficient matrix problem

\displaystyle\min_{\mathchar 29045\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 29006}}}\left\{\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29045\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28946\right\delimiter 0^{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12906{\cal\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\delimiter 84054785\text{ is rank deficient}\right\}\mathchar 24891

(61)

where
\mathchar 28946\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 29006}}
is a given point. Problem (
61
) is commonly known as the
structured total least squares
(STLS) problem
Rosen96SIMAA-stls
;
Markovsky07SP-stls
, and has numerous applications in control, systems theory, statistics
Markovsky08automatica-stls
, approximate greatest common divisor
Kaltofen06-approximateGCD
, camera triangulation
Aholt12eccv-qcqptriangulation
, among others
Markovsky14JCAM-slra
. Problem (
61
) can be reformulated as the following polynomial optimization problem

\min_{\mathchar 29050\mathchar 12850{\cal\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 29006}}}\left\{\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29045\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28946\right\delimiter 0^{\mathchar 28722}\ \middle\ {\mathchar 29050^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}}\left\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 28720}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29006}\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29033}\right\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\right\}\mathchar 24891

(STLS)

where
\mathchar 29004_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006
, are the set of independent bases of the affine map
{\cal\mathchar 29004}
,
\mathchar 29050\mathchar 12850{\cal\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
is a unit vector in the left kernel of
{\cal\mathchar 29004}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29045\delimiter 84054785
and acts as a witness of rank deficiency. Problem (
STLS
) is easily seen to be nonconvex and the best algorithm in practice is based on local nonlinear programming
Markovsky14JCAM-slra
.

Recently, Cifuentes
Cifuentes19arXiv-stls
devised a semidefinite relaxation for (
STLS
) and proved that the relaxation is guaranteed to be tight under a low-noise assumption
Cifuentes17arxiv-localstability
. This semidefinite relaxation is very similar to the relaxation for (
Wahba
) presented in Section
5.3
and is also a sparse second-order moment relaxation. Let
\mathchar 29048\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29050^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\delimiter 84267779^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 29028}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29028\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29006
be the vector of unknowns in (
STLS
), construct the sparse monomial vector of degree up to 2 in
\mathchar 29048
as

\displaystyle\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29050^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 29050^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29006}\mathchar 29050^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\delimiter 84267779^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 29038}}\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29006\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}

(62)

and build the moment matrix
\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}
. It can be easily checked that all the off-diagonal
\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}
blocks,
\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 29050\mathchar 29050^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}
, are symmetric by construction. Using
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}
, the
\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}
equality constraints in (
STLS
) can be conveniently written as
\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}
, for constant vectors
\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12850{{\mathbb{\mathchar 29010}}^{\mathchar 29038}}
. In addition, each of the equality constraint also gives rise to
\mathchar 29038
redundant constraints of the form
\delimiter 67273472\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 29025_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 84054785\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29048\delimiter 84267779_{\mathchar 29043}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
. Finally, the unit sphere constraint
\mathchar 29050\mathchar 12850{\cal\mathchar 29011}^{\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721}
implies the trace of the leading
\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8706\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}
block of
\mathchar 29016
is equal to 1. The construction above leads to a semidefinite relaxation with size

\displaystyle\mathchar 29038\mathchar 12349\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29006\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 29037\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29038\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8235\mathfrak{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 8706\mathfrak{\mathchar 29044}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 29006\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 314

(63)

Due to the limitation of interior point methods, Cifuentes
Cifuentes19arXiv-stls
was only able to numerically verify the tightness of the relaxation for very small problems (
e.g.,

~\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12820\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 12820\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12604\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28720
).

We aim to compute globally optimal solutions of (
STLS
) with much larger dimensions. We perform experiments on random instances of (
STLS
) where the affine map
{\cal\mathchar 29004}
is structured to be a square Hankel matrix such that
\mathchar 29006\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}\mathchar 8704\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28722}
. We set
\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12850\{\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28720\}
, and at each level we randomly generate three problem instances by drawing
\mathchar 28946\mathchar 12824{\cal\mathchar 29006}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 29006}\delimiter 84054785
from the standard Gaussian distribution. We then solve the sparse semidefinite relaxation using
SDPT3
,
MOSEK
,
CDCS
,
SketchyCGAL
,
SDPNAL+
, and
STRIDE
. For
STRIDE
, since the nonlinear programing (
STLS
) does not admit any manifold structure, we use
fmincon
with an interior point method as the
nlp
method. To generate hypotheses for
nlp
from the moment matrix
\mathchar 29016
, we follow

\displaystyle\mathchar 29016\mathchar 12349\mathchar 4944\displaylimits_{\mathchar 29033\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721}^{\mathchar 29038}\mathchar 28949_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}\mathchar 12349{{\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29050\delimiter 84267779\over\left\delimiter 0\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29050\delimiter 84267779\right\delimiter 0}}\mathchar 24891\quad\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 29033}_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}^{\mathsf{\mathchar 29012}}\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}\delimiter 67482370\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 29050\delimiter 84267779\mathchar 24891\;\mathchar 29034\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 24891\dots\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29006\mathchar 24891

(64)

where we first perform a spectral decomposition, then round
\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}
by projecting the entries of
\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}
corresponding to block
\mathchar 29050
onto the unit sphere, and round
\mathchar 29045^{\mathchar 29033}_{\mathchar 29034}
by computing the inner product between
\mathchar 29050_{\mathchar 29033}
and the entries of
\delimiter 69640972_{\mathchar 29033}
corresponding to block
\mathchar 29045_{\mathchar 29034}\mathchar 29050
(again, the rationale for this rounding comes from the lifting (
62
)). We generate
\mathchar 29042\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28725
hypotheses by rounding
\mathchar 28725
eigenvectors. In order to set
\mathchar 29005_{\mathchar 29026}
for computing
\mathchar 28945_{\mathchar 29043}
, we make the assumption that the search variable
\mathchar 29045
of (
STLS
) contains random vectors that follow
{\cal\mathchar 29006}\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 28720\mathchar 24891\mathchar 29001_{\mathchar 29006}\delimiter 84054785
, and its squared norm follows a chi-square distribution of degree
\mathchar 29006
. As a result, we choose the quantile corresponding to a
\mathchar 28729\mathchar 28729\mathchar 314\mathchar 28729\%
probability, denoted as
\overline{\mathchar 29005}
, as the bound on
\left\delimiter 0\mathchar 29045\right\delimiter 0^{\mathchar 28722}
, such that
\mathchar 29005_{\mathchar 29026}\mathchar 12349\overline{\mathchar 29005}\mathchar 8235\mathchar 28721
can upper bound the trace of
\mathchar 29016
.

Table
4
gives the numerical results of different solvers. Notice that at
\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28720
, we have increased the maximum runtime of
CDCS
and
SDPNAL+
to
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720\mathchar 28720
seconds to make a fair comparison with
STRIDE
. Generally, Table
4
has similar results as Table
3
. (i) IPMs can solve small problems to high accuracy, but cannot handle large problems due to memory issues. (ii) First-order solvers (
CDCS
and
SketchyCGAL
) are memory efficient but exhibit slow convergence and cannot attain high accuracy to certify global optimality and tightness of the relaxation.
SDPNAL+
can solve problems with
\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28721\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 28722\mathchar 28720
to high accuracy, but failed for
\mathchar 29006_{\mathchar 28721}\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28723\mathchar 28720
and
\mathchar 28724\mathchar 28720
. The degraded performance of
CDCS
,
SketchyCGAL
, and
SDPNAL+
compared to Tables
1
-
2
again suggests the difficulty in solving sparse relaxations compared to dense relaxations. (iii)
STRIDE
computed solutions of high accuracy for all test instances and it is about
\mathchar 28725
times faster than
SDPNAL+
.

6
Concluding Remarks

In this paper, we have designed an efficient algorithmic framework,
STRIDE
, to solve large-scale tight semidefinite programming (SDP) relaxations of polynomial optimization problems (POPs).
STRIDE
employs an inexact projected gradient method (iPGM) as its backbone, but leverages fast nonlinear programming (NLP) methods to seek rapid primal acceleration. By conducting a novel convergence analysis for the iPGM and taking safeguarded steps, we have proved the global convergence of
STRIDE
. For solving the projection subproblem in every iPGM step, we have designed a modified limited-memory BFGS method and proved its global convergence. In addition to the contributions in algorithmic design and convergence analysis, we have studied several important classes of POPs and their dense or sparse (tight) relaxations. In our extensive numerical experiments,
STRIDE
globally solved all the POPs and the corresponding SDP relaxations to very high accuracy, and it offered state-of-the-art efficiency and robustness. We hope the practical performance of
STRIDE
can encourage more theoretical study towards investigating when and why the NLP iterates can produce effective acceleration to the iPGM backbone.

Acknowledgements.

The authors would like to thank Diego Cifuentes for useful discussions about the nearest structured rank deficient matrix problem, and Jie Wang for clarifications about sparse semidefinite relaxations. H. Yang and L. Carlone were partially funded by ARL DCIST CRA W911NF-17-2-0181 and NSF CAREER award “Certifiable Perception for Autonomous Cyber-Physical Systems”.

, and this contradicts our initial hypothesis that
\mathchar 626\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 28952}\delimiter 84054785\mathrel{{{{\mathchar 566\mathchar 61}}}}\mathchar 28720
.

At this point, we have shown that
\mathchar 626\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\hat{\mathchar 28952}\delimiter 84054785\mathchar 12349\mathchar 28720
, as desired. At last, by the convexity of
\mathchar 28958\delimiter 67273472\mathchar 8705\delimiter 84054785
, vanishing of the gradient at
\hat{\mathchar 28952}
implies that
\hat{\mathchar 28952}
is an optimal solution of problem (
39
). Therefore, the proof is completed. ∎
